  - If A is inside B, the direction is A→B (within).
  - If the direction is B→A, then the relation is B relative to A (not within/contains).

--- Step 2: Entity Validation (warnings) ---
  - OSM geometry for Entity A (city, Polygon) is reliable.
  - OSM geometry for Entity B (protected_area, MultiPolygon) is reliable.
  No entity-level geometry conflicts with the context.

  - No explicit "in", "within", "contains", or "adjacent to" terminology.
  - No mention of shared boundary, partial enclosure, or identical footprint.

  - A bbox (34.077, 34.179, -117.637, -117.479) and B bbox (34.140, 34.753, -118.835, -117.584) partially overlap — this is NOT proof of polygon intersection.

**Key Points to Remember**

1. **Vernacular Priority** – If the sentence text (e.g., “part of its boundary”) contradicts the bbox or centroid data, choose the verbatim textual relation.
2. **Bounding‑Box Caution** – Overlap or containment in the bbox is *not proof* of the same topological relation for the polygons.
3. **Distance Gap** – A centroid distance of < 0.01 deg (~1 km) does NOT imply disjoint; many administrative entities share exact borders with 0 m gap.
4. **Entity Validation** – If the OSM geometry does not match the context (e.g., a river centroid far outside a city), treat the geometry as unreliable.
5. **Final Predicate** – Pick EXACTLY ONE from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.  End with: `Answer: [predicate]`.
